# Chapter 1: The heart of a demon never has regret even in death

"Fang Yuan, hand over the Spring Autumn Cicada and I'll give you a quick death!"

"Fang Yuan, you damn demon, you killed thousands just to cultivate the Spring Autumn Cicada. Your heinous sins are unforgivable!"

Fang Yuan's deep green robes were torn to shreds, his hair disheveled and his body covered in blood. He looked around.

Looking at the setting sun, Fang Yuan laughed softly. "The sun sets above the blue mountain, the autumn moon with the spring wind. Morning fine as hair, night like snow; success or failure, looking back, nothing remains."

Originally a scholar who crossed over from Earth, he had endured over five hundred grueling years in the blink of an eye.

"I failed in the end," Fang Yuan sighed inwardly, without any regret.

To be a demon was to be merciless and destructive, an enemy to the world with no place in heaven or earth, destined to face the consequences.

"If the Spring Autumn Cicada I just cultivated works, I'll still be a demon in my next life!" At that thought, Fang Yuan laughed aloud.

As the warlords surged forward, a loud bang erupted, engulfing Fang Yuan in a blinding surge of energy.

The spring rain quietly rained down on Qing Mao Mountain.

Situated on the mountain, Gu Yue Village brought a touch of human civilization to the vast, lonely wilderness.

At the center of Gu Yue Village stood a magnificent pavilion, radiant with bright lights as a grand ceremony took place within.

"Ancestors, please bless us! May this ceremony reveal talented, intelligent youths to bring new blood and hope to our clan!" The Gu Yue clan head, a middle-aged man with graying sideburns in ceremonial white robes, knelt on the floor and prayed sincerely.

When the ceremony ended, the crowd rose from the ground and silently exited the sacred temple.

As the elders walked out together, one sighed, "Bai Village and Xiong Village have produced talented geniuses these past few years. Especially Bai Ning Bing of the Bai clan—his natural talent is terrifying."

With splendid talent, the youth had already reached rank three Gu Master after just two years of training.

Another elder nodded, "Still, Fang Zhi's line produced a young genius who recited poetry at five. Exceptionally intelligent, though orphaned early and raised by his aunt and uncle."

He knew immediately that the elders were discussing the youth named Gu Yue Fang Yuan.

As clan head, he naturally paid attention to prominent youths, and Gu Yue Fang Yuan was the most eye-catching among them.

With A grade potential, he could rival Bai Ning Bing; even with B grade, he would become a pillar of the Gu Yue clan. Given such precocious intelligence, an A grade was far more likely than a B grade.

At that very moment, a pair of clear eyes watched the sparkling lights with deep, complex emotions.

"Gu Yue Village, five hundred years ago? Looks like the Spring Autumn Cicada really worked..." Fang Yuan stood by the window, letting the wind-blown rain hit him.

The Spring Autumn Cicada could reverse time. Ranked seventh among the Ten Great Mystical Gu, it was no ordinary creature.

Listening to the soft drizzle against the windowsill, he closed his eyes. "Five hundred years of experience... it really feels like a dream," he sighed.

# Chapter 2: Going back in time with 500 years of knowledge

Legend held that a River of Time sustained the world's flow. By using the Spring Autumn Cicada's power, one could travel upstream into the past.

Fang Yuan sighed. Despite his rebirth, the Spring Autumn Cicada had not accompanied him.

Humans are the spirit of all living beings; Gu are the essence of heaven and earth.

"Even if it is gone, I can refine another. I did it in my past life, so why not this one?" Setting aside his regret, ambition and resolve surged within Fang Yuan.

His greatest treasure was five hundred years of memories and experience.

In his memories were countless unopened treasures of this era, and he could easily grasp major events along the currents of history. He remembered countless hidden experts and rising geniuses, along with five hundred years of rich combat experience.

"Cultivation is paramount. I haven't even opened my primeval sea or stepped onto the Gu Master path—I'm just a mortal! I must cultivate quickly to seize every historical opportunity."

"To cultivate as quickly as possible, I must rely on clan resources. In my current state, I cannot cross dangerous mountains—even an ordinary mountain boar could kill me. Only by reaching rank three will I have the strength to protect myself and leave this mountain."

Just then, the door was pushed open and a youth walked in.

"Brother, why are you standing by the window in the rain?"

The thin youth was slightly shorter than Fang Yuan, with nearly identical features.

"So it's you, my twin brother." Fang Yuan raised an eyebrow, his expression returning to cold indifference. Fang Zheng lowered his head toward his toes in his signature stance.

"Brother, I saw your window was open and came to close it. Tomorrow is the Awakening Ceremony and it's late. If Uncle and Aunt find out you haven't slept, they'll worry."

Fang Zheng was not surprised by Fang Yuan's coldness; his brother had always been like that. He often wondered if geniuses were simply different from ordinary people.

Born from the same womb at the same time, why was heaven so unfair? His brother was endowed with gleaming talent, while he was as ordinary as a stone.

"Worried..." Fang Yuan laughed silently at the thought of his aunt and uncle. Their parents had died on a clan mission when he was only three, leaving both brothers orphaned.

Under the pretext of raising them, their aunt and uncle seized their parents' inheritance while treating both brothers harshly.

He had originally intended to hide his abilities and bide his time, but hardship forced Fang Yuan to reveal some of his talent.

Under widespread attention, young Fang Yuan maintained a cold, indifferent demeanor to protect his secrets, which eventually became an ingrained habit.

Consequently, his aunt and uncle softened their treatment. As he grew, promising better prospects, they treated him increasingly well—not out of love, but as an investment.

Hilariously, his younger brother never saw the truth; deceived by their aunt and uncle, he only harbored buried resentment. Though he seemed honest and good-natured now, Fang Yuan remembered that once his brother was revealed as an A grade talent, the clan poured everything into raising him. With all that buried jealousy and hatred unleashed, Fang Zheng would repeatedly target, suppress, and make life difficult for his older brother.

As for his own grade, it was only C grade talent.

In his previous life, Fang Yuan hated his lack of talent and the heartless clan. But with five hundred years of experience, his heart was entirely calm, without hatred.

The strong preyed on the weak—survival of the fittest had always been the rule of this world. Everyone struggled for their ambitions; amid endless bloodshed, what was there not to understand?

Whoever blocked his pursuit of immortality, he would kill. This path made the world his enemy, leaving him destined to walk alone.

"Revenge is not my intention; the demonic path does not compromise." Chuckling, he glanced faintly at his brother. "You may leave."

"Then I'll see you tomorrow." Not daring to say anymore, Fang Zheng slowly closed the door and left.

# Chapter 3: Please go aside and scram

He had slept barely two hours after devising plans through the night, leaving his uncultivated body weighed with exhaustion.

Yet with five hundred years of experience, Fang Yuan possessed iron will; such mild exhaustion was nothing to him.

Shoving aside the thin silk blanket, he rose neatly and opened the window to find the spring rain had ceased.

"Young Master Fang Yuan, you're awake. I'll come up to help you wash," a young maid called from downstairs.

Looking down, Fang Yuan saw his own personal servant—Shen Cui.

This servant girl was merely his aunt and uncle's spy. In his past life, she had captivated him, only to treat him with disdain once his status plummeted.

"No need to tidy up." Fang Yuan brushed aside Shen Cui's soft hands. His clothes were already neat; she was merely trying to seduce him.

"You can leave." Without looking at her, Fang Yuan straightened his cuffs. Shen Cui pouted, finding his cold behavior puzzling and upsetting. She wanted to act spoiled, but intimidated by his cold demeanor, she merely murmured an agreement and retreated obediently.

"Are you ready?" Fang Yuan asked Fang Zheng.

Fang Zheng stood at the doorway staring at his toes, muttering an assent with dark circles ringing his sleepless eyes.

In his past life he had been blind to his brother's thoughts, but now he understood completely. "Then let's go," he said lightly.

The two brothers left the house, encountering many peers in small groups along the way, all heading toward the same destination.

"Outstanding talent truly invites jealousy," Fang Yuan sneered inwardly as he listened to the surrounding gossip.

He recalled his aunt and uncle's scheming—assigning Shen Cui to spy on him while giving his brother an old wet nurse, alongside countless other disparities.

With the Awakening Ceremony already upon him, the situation seemed set; yet with Fang Yuan's demonic wisdom, changing it was no impossible feat.

He could easily suppress his brother, take Shen Cui as a concubine, and defeat his aunt, uncle, and clan elders in hundreds of ways.

"But, I don't feel like doing that…" Fang Yuan sighed carefreely.

As long as you do not block my way, you can scram aside; I need not care about you.

# Chapter 4: Gu Yue Fang Yuan

Over a hundred fifteen-year-old youths gathered before the heavily guarded five-story clan pavilion at the village center.

"Good, all of you are punctual. Today is the Awakening Ceremony, a major turning point in your lives. Follow me," said the academy elder. White-haired and in high spirits, he led the teenagers past the great hall and downward instead of upstairs. Descending a stone staircase, they entered an underground cave.

Fang Yuan observed from the crowd, recalling how the Gu Yue clan had migrated from the Central Continent to Qing Mao Mountain centuries ago. They had discovered a spirit spring in this cave that produced abundant primeval stones, serving as the foundation of Gu Yue Village.

"When your name is called, cross the river to the opposite bank. Walk as far as you can; the further you go, the better. Are you all clear?" the elder asked.

"Gu Yue Chen Bo," the elder called from the roster.

The river was wide but knee-deep. Looking solemn, Chen Bo waded across and stepped into the sea of flowers ashore. He felt an invisible wall of pressure blocking his path. At that moment, the flowers at his feet began emitting a faint white light. The light entered Chen Bo's body, easing the pressure so he could push forward against the softened barrier. He forced himself forward, but after just three steps the barrier hardened again, preventing him from advancing any further.

The elder sighed and recorded the result. "Gu Yue Chen Bo, three steps, no talent to become a Gu Master. Next, Gu Yue Zao Xie!"

After two consecutive failures, the observing elders looked grim and the clan head frowned. Then, the academy elder called the third youth: Gu Yue Mo Bei.

"Here!" A horse-faced youth in linen robes answered. Tall, sturdy, and carrying a bold aura, he crossed the river in several strides to the opposite bank. Ten steps, twenty, thirty—motes of light poured into him until he finally stopped at thirty-six steps.

The watching youths stared in shock. The academy elder beamed, "Good! Gu Yue Mo Bei, B grade talent! Come forward and let me inspect your primeval sea."

The elder placed a hand on Mo Bei's shoulder and nodded, recording: Gu Yue Mo Bei, sixty-six percent primeval sea, worthy of intensive nurturing.

Talent spanned four grades from A to D. A D grade could reach rank one in three years, forming a family's foundation. A C grade could become a rank two Gu Master, while a nurtured B grade formed the future elders.

An A grade brought great fortune to the clan, capable of reaching rank four in ten years to compete for clan head!

An hour later, half the youths had walked through the flower sea. Many showed C and D grade talent, while half had none at all.

"Sigh, the bloodline is thinning. In recent years the clan hasn't had any rank four masters to strengthen it. Our only rank five master perished alongside the Flower Wine Monk. The clan's talents grow weaker with each generation," the clan head sighed.

At this moment, the academy elder shouted, "Gu Yue Chi Cheng!"

Reaching the opposite bank, the lights entered his body; he stopped after walking thirty-six steps.

"Another B grade!" the academy elder yelled.

"Gu Yue Chi Cheng, huh…" In the crowd, Fang Yuan stroked his chin. In his memory, the clan heavily punished Chi Cheng for cheating in the Awakening Ceremony. Chi Cheng only had C grade talent, but his grandfather Gu Yue Chi Lian helped him fake a B grade result.

Chi Cheng was different; his grandfather Gu Yue Chi Lian was one of the clan's two most authoritative elders, capable of covering for him.

"Gu Yue Chi Lian and Gu Yue Mo Chen are the clan's two chief rivals. To suppress his opponent, Chi Lian needed his grandson to display outstanding talent. With his grandfather's backing, Chi Cheng concealed the truth for a time. In my memories, if not for that incident, it would never have been exposed."

Fang Yuan's eyes gleamed, scheming how to turn this knowledge to his advantage.

Exposing it now would yield only a minor reward while offending the powerful Gu Yue Chi Lian.

As he pondered, the academy elder called out, "Gu Yue Fang Yuan!"

# Chapter 5: The First Human and 3 Gu, Hopes Awakening

It's getting more and more exciting, Fang Yuan chuckled inwardly. Under the crowd's gaze, he crossed the river to the opposite bank.

Soon, specks of light rose from the flowers beneath his feet, enveloping his body before entering him.

These are Hope Gu, Fang Yuan mused, recognizing every speck of light.

An ancient legend spoke of Hope Gu. When the savage world was roamed by wild beasts, the first human, Ren Zu, was hunted by beasts called Predicament.

At that moment, three Gu approached him: "As long as you nourish us with your life, we will help you through this difficulty." With nowhere else to turn, Ren Zu agreed.

He gave his youth to the largest Gu, which granted him strength in return.

With strength, Ren Zu secured food and defeated many Predicaments, but soon realized strength was not everything.

Bitterly reflecting on this lesson, Ren Zu offered his prime middle years to the most beautiful Gu, which granted him wisdom.

Yet Ren Zu aged rapidly after surrendering his youth and middle age; his muscles withered and his mind slowed.

"Human, you have nothing left to offer us," Strength Gu and Wisdom Gu said upon realizing this, abandoning him.

As he collapsed in despair, surrounded by Predicaments, the third Gu spoke: "Human, take me. I will help you escape Predicament."

Ren Zu wept, "Gu, I have nothing left. Strength and Wisdom have abandoned me; I only have my old age! I want to live a little longer, even just a second more. Leave—I have nothing left to give you."

The Gu replied, "My needs are the smallest. Human, just give me your heart, and it will be enough."

When Ren Zu gave it his heart, the Gu radiated brilliant light. The Predicaments shrieked in terror: "Hope Gu! Retreat! We Predicaments fear hope most!"

The Predicaments fled. From that day on, whenever facing a predicament, Ren Zu gave his heart to hope.

The Hope Gu converged into a stream of light inside Fang Yuan's body. Driven by the external pressure, they clustered three inches below his navel.

With each step, Hope Gu rose from the flower sea into his body, brightening the orb of light as the elder frowned.

"Fewer Hope Gu than expected," the elders thought. This was certainly no sign of an A grade talent.

"Under ten steps means no talent; ten to twenty is D grade; twenty to thirty is C; thirty to forty is B; forty to fifty is A. I'm at twenty-three steps so far."

24, 25, 26… 27.

At the twenty-seventh step, the orb of light between his kidneys reached its limit and exploded with a bang.

Focusing inward, Fang Yuan saw an aperture had formed out of thin air below his navel.

The Awakening Ceremony was a success!

# Chapter 6: The road to the future will be interesting

The aperture was wondrous; though inside his body, it did not share physical space with his organs. It was both infinitely vast and infinitely small.

Within the light membrane lay the Primeval Sea—its waters smooth as a mirror, greenish-blue, and dense with a copper luster. Only Rank one Gu Masters formed this essence, known as the green copper sea.

The sea filled only 44% of the aperture, the limit of a C grade talent, condensing pure primeval essence.

With his aperture opened, Fang Yuan had formally stepped onto the path of a Rank one Gu Master, and no more Hope Gu entered him.

The pressure ahead was now like a solid wall; he could not advance another step. "Just like my previous life," Fang Yuan smiled indifferently.

"You can't go any further?" the academy elder shouted across the river, clinging to faint hope. Fang Yuan simply turned and walked back.

"What? Fang Yuan walked 27 steps?"

"So he was just a C grade talent?!"

"Brother…" Fang Zheng watched in disbelief as Fang Yuan returned. How could his brother only have C grade talent?

"Damn, only a C grade!" The Gu Yue clan head clenched his fists, breathing deeply in disappointment.

Seeing the scene replay, Fang Yuan remained calm. He recalled the legend: when Predicaments come, give your heart to hope.

Walk your own path; let others be disappointed as they please!

The academy elder sighed deeply and called out, "Next, Gu Yue Fang Zheng!"

"Ah? Coming!" Fang Zheng rushed forward, only to trip over his own feet and tumble into the river.

Instantly the entire cave was filled with huge laughter.

Just then, a strong pull hauled him upright, lifting his head from the water.

Wiping his face in a panic, he saw Fang Yuan holding his collar, having pulled him up.

At a loss, Fang Zheng heard his brother say, "Go on. The road ahead will be interesting."

Fang Zheng stared in surprise, catching the knowing smile on his brother's lips and feeling his profound calm.

Bewildered by his brother's calm, Fang Zheng could only watch as Fang Yuan patted his back and walked away.

Lowering his head, Fang Zheng waded forward in a daze until he stood deep in the flower sea at an unprecedented distance.

43 steps!

"An A grade talent!" the academy elder shrieked, beside himself.

"After three years, an A grade genius has finally appeared in the Gu Yue clan!"

"The Fang branch originated from the Chi bloodline. Our Chi family will adopt Fang Zheng," Gu Yue Chi Lian announced.

"Nonsense! Chi Lian, you old fool, give the boy to me to raise!" Gu Yue Mo Chen roared back.

"Silence! No one is more qualified to raise this child than the clan head!" Gu Yue Bo glared fiercely around the hall.

# Chapter 7: A Gu Master has 9 ranks, Flower Wine leaves behind treasure

Soon a week passed.

"Humans are the spirit of all living things; Gu are the essence of heaven and earth. Countless species of Gu thrive everywhere—in soil, vegetation, and upon wild beasts."

"As humanity grew, ancient sages unraveled Gu mysteries. Those who open their apertures to feed, refine, and command Gu with their primeval essence are known as Gu Masters."

"Having opened your apertures seven days ago and formed your Primeval Seas, you are all now Rank one Gu Masters."

In the academy, the elder lectured with composure before fifty-seven attentive students.

A youth raised his hand and, upon receiving permission, asked, "Elder, we know there are Rank one, Rank two, and higher Gu Masters—could you explain the ranks in more detail?"

The Gu Yue teacher nodded and waved his hand to ask the young man to sit down. "Gu Masters have 9 ranks, from bottom to top—Rank one, Rank two, Rank three all the way up to Rank nine. Each rank is a major realm divided into four minor realms: initial, middle, upper, and peak stage. As newly awakened Gu Masters, you are all at Rank one initial stage."

"For D grade talent, the Primeval Sea takes up about 2-3 layers of the aperture, the highest promotion reachable is Rank one to Rank two. For C grade talent, the Primeval Sea is 4-5 layers of the aperture. Such exceptional talent is best suited for cultivation, capable of reaching Rank five."

"Gu Masters of Rank six and above are legendary. Our clan has never produced a Rank six, though we have had Rank four and five masters."

By the corner window, Gu Yue Fang Yuan slumped over his desk, fast asleep.

Continuing his lecture, the elder said, "Our clan has produced two Rank five masters. The first was our ancestral first generation clan head. He established the village. The other was the fourth generation clan head, who also reached Rank five. Had that shameless demon Flower Wine Monk not ambushed him, he might have even reached Rank six..."

"Flower Wine Monk, huh..." Awoken by the chatter, Fang Yuan opened his eyes.

He stretched, brooding over why he hadn't found the monk's legacy despite scouring the village.

In his memories, a heartbroken clan Gu Master would turn to heavy drinking two months from now; his drunken stupor outside the village would lure a Liquor worm with the aroma of wine.

Chasing the precious worm into a hidden cave, the Gu Master discovered the Flower Wine Monk's remains and inheritance.

For days Fang Yuan had bought wine and roamed the village at night, hoping the scent would lure the Liquor worm, but found nothing.

"Refining that Liquor worm into my vital Gu would be far better than the clan's Moonlight Gu. It's already April; time is running out." Fang Yuan sighed, gazing out the window.

As class ended, the elder announced, "You have learned to inspect your Primeval Sea and circulate primeval essence. Now you must refine your vital Gu. Head to the Gu room after class, pick a Gu worm, and return home to refine it. Only after succeeding may you return to class. This is your first assessment—the first to finish will receive twenty primeval stones."

# Chapter 8: Things will always be things, but humans will change

Beside the academy lay the Gu room, spanning only sixty square meters.

"Line up and enter one by one!" the guards shouted. The youths entered sequentially until it was finally Fang Yuan's turn.

Square recesses of varying sizes lined the four walls, holding different Gu worms.

"Gu are divided into nine ranks matching Gu Masters; all the Gu here are Rank one." Fang Yuan noted as he looked around.

A Gu Master's first refined Gu was of paramount importance—it became their vital Gu, linked to their very life, inflicting severe backlash if killed.

"I originally hoped to refine the Flower Wine Monk's Liquor worm as my vital Gu, but I still have no leads on his remains. I don't know when it will be found, so I'll pick a Moonlight Gu first to be safe." Fang Yuan sighed inwardly, walking along the left wall.

Shaped like a crescent of blue quartz, the crystalline Gu rested against the silver dish, exuding a quiet beauty.

Known as the Moonlight Gu, it was the Gu Yue clan's specialty, chosen by many clansmen as their vital Gu.

Since these were all Rank one Moonlight Gu with little difference between them, Fang Yuan casually took one. It felt as light as paper.

Others would have rushed home to refine their Gu immediately, but Fang Yuan was in no hurry, his mind still on the Liquor worm.

Leaving the Gu room, Fang Yuan headed straight for the tavern.

"Shopkeeper, two jars of aged wine!" Fang Yuan fished out his remaining primeval stone pieces and placed them on the counter.

Primeval stones were the world's currency, measuring commodity value while containing condensed essence essential for a Gu Master's cultivation.

"Two jars a day for seven full days. My savings are nearly gone," Fang Yuan thought with a slight frown, leaving the tavern with both jars.

Carrying the two jars, he wondered, "The academy elder said the first person to refine their vital Gu will receive twenty primeval stones. With my C-grade talent and no special methods, I have no chance of winning."

Fang Zheng's voice called from behind, "Brother, you really did buy wine! Follow me, Aunt and Uncle want to see you."

Fang Yuan stopped and turned. His younger brother no longer kept his head lowered, meeting his eyes directly.

Seeing Fang Yuan disheveled with wine jars, Fang Zheng felt relieved, yet urged, "Brother, you have to stop drinking—you can't go on like this! You have no idea how worried everyone is. You need to wake up!"

Fang Yuan remained expressionless and silent, meeting his brother's gaze.

Fang Zheng's gaze sharpened, but Fang Yuan walked past him, saying flatly, "What are you staring at? Let's go."

Seeing his brother pull ahead, Fang Zheng quickened his pace. Holding his head high toward the sun, his eyes fixed on his own feet stepping onto Fang Yuan's shadow.

# Chapter 9: Two people who start on the same road, gradually becoming distant

In the dimly lit living room, Aunt and Uncle sat high in their chairs, their expressions hidden in shadow.

Seeing Fang Yuan carrying two wine jars, Uncle Gu Yue Dong Tu's brows furrowed. "In the blink of an eye, you are both fifteen now. Since you both possess Gu Master talent—especially Fang Zheng—your aunt and I are proud of you. Take these six primeval stones each. Refining your Gu consumes much primeval essence, so you will need them."

Servants stepped forward and handed Fang Yuan and Fang Zheng each a small pouch.

Fang Zheng opened his pouch to find six oval, grayish-white primeval stones. Beaming with gratitude, he stood up to face his aunt and uncle. "Thank you, Aunt and Uncle! You raised me, and I will never forget this kindness!"

Uncle nodded with a smile, while Aunt waved warmly. "Sit down! Though you aren't our birth children, we raised you as our own. Seeing you secure a future makes us proud. We have no children of our own, and have often wished you two could truly be ours."

Uncle chimed in, "Your aunt and I wish to adopt you both into a real family. Fang Zheng, are you willing?"

Fang Zheng beamed with joy. "Ever since my parents died, I have longed for a family. Becoming your son is a dream come true!"

Aunt beamed and laughed, "As our good son, shouldn't you change how you address us?"

"Father, Mother," Fang Zheng corrected himself in realization.

Uncle looked at the silent Fang Yuan and said gently, "Fang Yuan, how about you?"

Fang Yuan shook his head without saying a word.

"Brother—" Fang Zheng started to protest, but Uncle smoothly cut him off. "In that case, nephew Fang Yuan, we won't force you. At fifteen, you should learn independence to carry on the Fang bloodline. I have prepared two hundred primeval stones to support you."

But Fang Yuan still shook his head.

"If there is nothing else, I will take my leave." Grabbing his wine jars, Fang Yuan walked out.

"Your son takes his leave as well," Fang Zheng said, retreating as the room fell silent.

As the sun set and darkness filled the room, Uncle's cold voice broke the silence. "It seems that brat Fang Yuan saw through our plan."

Clan regulations stipulated that an eldest son could inherit family property at sixteen. Fang Yuan's deceased parents had left a fortune, which Aunt and Uncle were currently managing. That inheritance far exceeded two hundred primeval stones, but adoption would forfeit his claim.

"Fortunately, we secured Fang Zheng, and Fang Yuan only has C-grade talent," Uncle sighed in relief.

"Husband, what if he claims independence at sixteen?" Aunt asked anxiously, fixated on the inheritance.

"If he won't cooperate, we'll frame him for a major offense and expel him before then, forfeiting his inheritance," Uncle explained coldly.

"The boy is clever—why would he make a mistake?" Aunt asked, puzzled.

Uncle hissed, "We have Shen Cui seduce him and cry assault, blaming it on drunken misconduct. That will easily get him expelled."

Matron Shen was their trusted head housekeeper in charge of all household servants, and Shen Cui, Fang Yuan's maid, was her daughter.

"Aunt and Uncle are likely plotting to frame and expel me, just like in my past life."

"All beginnings are difficult, especially for me. I lack exceptional talent and have no mentor to guide me. In my past life, Aunt and Uncle stole my inheritance, costing me two full years just to reach Rank one peak stage. I cannot repeat that mistake."

Carrying the two wine jars, he headed toward the village outskirts instead of going home.

Mountain rain approached, but with inheritance locked until sixteen, the Flower Wine Monk's treasure was his only immediate hope.

# Chapter 10: A storm may arise from a clear sky, refining Gu is full of hardships

In the dim room, Fang Yuan watched the heavy downpour through the open window and sighed.

"It has already been 3 days and 3 nights."

Three nights ago, he had set out with wine to search the outskirts, but a downpour halted his search.

"It seems I won't find the Liquor worm anytime soon. To be safe, I should begin refining the Moonlight Gu. Who in this world enjoys a journey free of obstacles?"

Closing the door and window, he sat cross-legged on his bed and calmed his breathing.

His primeval aperture appeared in his mind's eye—boundless yet minute, enveloped in a thin layer of white light.

Inside lay a copper-green Primeval Sea, filling forty-four percent of the aperture.

Every drop of primeval essence was precious—the root of a Gu Master's power, required to refine and activate Gu.

Withdrawing his mind, Fang Yuan opened his eyes and retrieved the Moonlight Gu. It rested in his palm like a small, crystalline crescent moon.

A jet of primeval essence surged into the Moonlight Gu. It flared with blue light and trembled, resisting the influx.

Gu embody the essence of heaven and earth, bearing natural laws and born with wills of their own. Refining the Moonlight Gu required erasing its will, so the insect instinctively resisted the imminent danger.

As copper-green primeval essence poured into the crescent, its two pointed tips turned green, the color slowly spreading toward the center.

Twenty minutes later, his sea fell to fourteen percent, having turned only one-twelfth of the Gu green.

Even after he stopped, the Moonlight Gu steadily expelled his primeval essence.

At this rate, the Gu would completely purge his primeval essence within six hours, forcing him to start over from scratch.

"Refining Gu is like positional warfare—a battle of attrition. Refining just one-twelfth consumed three-quarters of my primeval essence. A Gu Master must replenish essence while continuously refining to consolidate gains. It tests both essence control and endurance."

Fang Yuan retrieved a primeval stone from his pouch as he pondered.

Absorbing essence from the stone, his sea returned to forty-four percent—the limit of his C-grade talent.

To make lasting progress, Fang Yuan had to rely on primeval stones, as his natural recovery could not outpace the Moonlight Gu's expulsion rate.

Without needing primeval stones, Fang Zheng could refine his Gu simply by resting.

"I only have six primeval stones. I cannot compete with peers like Gu Yue Mo Bei or Gu Yue Chi Cheng, who have elders backing them. With C-grade talent compared to Fang Zheng's A-grade, I never had a chance to win this contest. Better to focus on finding the Liquor worm. Making the Liquor worm my vital Gu would be vastly superior to the Moonlight Gu. Hmm? The rain outside seems to be easing up. It has rained for three full days and nights; it should stop soon."

Stowing the Moonlight Gu, Fang Yuan got off the bed. Just as he went to open the window, someone knocked.

Maid Shen Cui called from outside, "Young Master Fang Yuan, I brought food and wine to cheer you up."

# Chapter 11: It’s just power play

Fang Yuan frowned slightly. With five hundred years of experience, he immediately smelled a conspiracy.

His eyes flickered as he smoothed his brow. "I happen to be hungry. Come in."

Carrying the food basket, Shen Cui smiled coldly outside, but adopted a meek, gentle expression the moment she opened the door.

"Young Master Fang Yuan, the food and wine smell wonderful," she said sweetly, flattering him. Setting the basket on the table, she neatly arranged the dishes.

"Come sit, Young Master. I mustered my courage to drink with you." Smiling radiantly, she guided him to a chair.

Sitting on his lap and leaning against his chest, she whispered, "Young Master Fang Yuan, I have always loved you. No matter your aptitude, I want to stay by your side and comfort you. Tonight, I give myself to you."

"Let me feed you myself." Taking a sip from the cup, Shen Cui leaned toward his lips.

Fang Yuan remained indifferent, as though holding a stone sculpture rather than a young maiden.

Fang Yuan scoffed disdainfully, "So it's just a power play."

Shen Cui froze and swallowed the wine, forcing a flattering smile. "Young Master, whatever do you mean...?"

Gazing coldly, Fang Yuan gripped her neck and squeezed as she choked in terror. "Young Master, you're hurting me!"

"Uncle and Aunt sent you to frame me, didn't they?" Fang Yuan sneered. "Do you really think these meager charms give you the right to scheme against me?"

Shen Cui gasped greedily for air, coughing violently. Fang Yuan gave a light chuckle, gently caressing her cheek. "Shen Cui, do you think I can kill you, or not?"

"Because you served me for years, I will spare your life. If you want to escape servitude, go find my naive little brother."

"You can leave."

Stunned and terrified out of her wits, Shen Cui obediently stumbled out, barely comprehending how she had escaped the demon named Fang Yuan.

Hidden in the shadows, the waiting men looked on in confusion as Shen Cui emerged visibly shaken.

Fang Yuan departed shortly after. A wise person avoids known perils; staying under his aunt and uncle's roof was sheer folly.

Arriving at the village's only inn, Fang Yuan inquired, "Innkeeper, do you have any rooms available?"

Fang Yuan handed two primeval stones to the innkeeper. "Give me a good room, two jars of wine, and three or four dishes."

"Done." Taking the two primeval stones, the innkeeper asked, "Will you eat in your room, or in the hall?"

With rain stopped and evening approaching, Fang Yuan chose to eat in the hall before searching the outskirts. "I'll eat in the hall," he replied.

Three tables were occupied. By the window, a lone old man drank wine while watching the sunset. In the middle of the hall sat five or six hunters.

In another corner, two youths conferred in secret, their figures obscured by shadows so that even their genders were difficult to discern.

Fang Yuan sat at the table nearest the door, and his dishes were served shortly after.

"With my C-grade talent, refining the Moonlight Gu will require at least five to eight primeval stones."

"I only had six primeval stones, and giving two to the innkeeper leaves me with four. It's not enough."

"Even if I find the Liquor worm, refining it will require even more stones. The monk's inheritance must hold what I need."

# Chapter 12: Green Bamboo Wine is fragrant, Gu Master flaunts power

"Everything hinges on the Flower Wine Monk's treasure; without it, my cultivation will crawl. If that happens, I'll fall behind my peers. I don't understand—I've spent over a week trying to lure the Liquor worm, so why hasn't it appeared?"

A loud noise interrupted him: the six hunters in the center of the hall were thoroughly drunk and rowdy.

"Brother Feng stopped drinking—is this wine not good enough? Waiter, bring us some good wine!"

As the drunken shouting grew louder, the waiter hurried over. "Good sirs, we have fine wine, but it is quite expensive."

The waiter quickly added, "I wouldn't dare look down on you brave men, but this wine costs two primeval stones a jar!"

The shouting faded as the hunters sank back, unable to afford such a steep price. A young man in the dark corner sneered, "Hilarious. Blustering loudly over nothing. If you can't afford wine, shut your mouths and step aside!"

An enraged hunter retorted, "Bring that wine! I'll pay the two primeval stones!"

"Right away, sir!" Caught off guard by this sudden turn of events, the waiter hurried to fetch a wine jar. The moment the jar was uncorked, a refreshing and mellow fragrance filled the entire hall.

"Guests, this is green bamboo wine, unique to our inn. Just smell that fragrance!" The waiter beamed.

Village taverns sold only common rice wine; this fragrant green bamboo wine was far superior.

The youth in the corner sneered, "How is one small jar enough for six? If you have the guts, buy a few more."

Furious, the hunter stood up and glared at the youth. "Brat, you have a big mouth. Come, stand up and fight me!"

"Then I will." Grinning, the young man stepped from the shadows in navy battle robes.

Around his waist was a green belt centered with a copper plate engraved with "One".

"A Rank one Gu Master?!" Recognizing the attire, the hunter drew a sharp breath, his anger instantly giving way to alarm.

"Didn't you want to fight? Hit me." The young Gu Master smiled playfully as the hunter stood frozen in terror.

The young Gu Master picked up the jar of green bamboo wine, sniffed it, and smiled. "It certainly smells good..."

"If my lord likes it, please take it as an apology for my offense," the hunter stammered, bowing nervously.

The youth smashed the jar onto the floor and hissed, "You think you have the right to apologize to me? You hunters spend two primeval stones on wine when I am desperate for stones myself! How dare you flaunt your wealth before me! Can you mortals even compare to me?!"

Only Fang Yuan watched quietly, void of hesitation.

The belt plate marked him as a Rank one Gu Master at upper stage despite being twenty.

Reaching only upper stage by twenty meant he had D-grade talent, one grade below Fang Yuan.

"Alright Jiang Ya, don't humiliate these mortals further—if word gets out, it's embarrassing," the other youth called out.

Flicking his sleeve, Jiang Ya strode back. "If anyone else has the guts to drink green bamboo wine, let's see it!"

Fang Yuan set down his chopsticks and placed two primeval stones on the table. "Waiter, give me a jar of green bamboo wine," he said calmly.

Jiang Ya froze, his mouth twitching at the immediate defiance.

He turned around, glaring coldly at Fang Yuan with narrowed eyes.

Fang Yuan stared back calmly, indifferent and unafraid.

Sensing Fang Yuan's primeval essence, Jiang Ya's gaze softened into a warm smile. "Ah, it's a junior brother."

The waiter scurried over with the wine. Fang Yuan nodded, took the jar, and left the inn.

# Chapter 13: The bamboo forest under the moon, a bead of snow

About three hundred years ago, a genius emerged in the Gu Yue clan, reaching Rank five at a young age with the potential to advance even further.

He died defending the clan against the demonic Rank five Flower Wine Monk, supposedly forcing him to beg for mercy.

However, a careless moment left him vulnerable to the Monk's sneak attack. Though the fourth clan leader executed him, he succumbed to his severe injuries.

This tragic tale was popular in the Gu Yue clan, but Fang Yuan knew better than to believe a story with such a glaring loophole.

In his previous life, a month from now, a lovelorn Gu Master lay dead drunk outside the village, where his strong scent of wine attracted a Liquor worm.

Pursuing the Liquor worm into a hidden underground cave, the Gu Master discovered the Flower Wine Monk's remains and inheritance, then reported it to the clan, causing a massive stir.

Walking through the bamboo forest at nightfall, Fang Yuan reviewed his clues. "Something about that story feels off."

"Putting myself in his shoes, why would anyone report the Flower Wine Monk's treasure instead of claiming it all? Clan honor aside, everyone harbors greed in their hearts. What could make that Gu Master abandon his greed, surrender all profit, and report his discovery to the clan's leadership?"

Fang Yuan thought of his situation. "Buying this jar leaves me with only two primeval stones. Tonight is my final gamble!"

Three days of rain had cleansed the mountain air, leaving a crisp freshness that carried the wine aroma far.

Fang Yuan broke the seal, releasing a rich aroma. Green bamboo wine was the village's finest—the third reason for his confidence.

"With all three factors aligned, tonight must be the night!" Fang Yuan thought, tilting the jar to drip a small stream of wine onto a rock.

The rich aroma spread into the night on a gentle breeze, permeating the bamboo forest. Fang Yuan waited in place, but saw no movement.

He repeated the process across several locations, dripping wine until only a little remained in the jar.

"This is the last time," Fang Yuan sighed, emptying the jar over the grass and wildflowers.

Clinging to a final shred of hope, Fang Yuan looked around.

"It failed after all," he muttered inwardly. "Even with three major advantages, I didn't even glimpse the Liquor worm. My future chances will only be lower. I only have two primeval stones left and must refine the Moonlight Gu. I can't risk any more."

A gust of wind swept aside the clouds, revealing the hidden moon.

Turning away, he suddenly spotted a dot of pure white.

Clinging to a nearby spear bamboo stalk, it resembled a round pearl suspended under the moonlight.

Fang Yuan's pupils dilated and his body trembled as his heart began to race.

It was the Liquor worm!

# Chapter 14: In the mountain crevice hides a profound theory

Shaped like a silkworm, the Liquor worm glowed with pearlescent white light, plump and adorable.

The Liquor worm fed on wine and flew at high speeds curled into a ball. Though only a Rank one Gu, it was worth more than several Rank two Gu.

As a vital Gu, it was far superior to the Moonlight Gu.

The Liquor worm clung to a bamboo stalk merely fifty paces away. Holding his breath, Fang Yuan slowly backed away.

If the Liquor worm took flight, he could never match its speed. He had to wait until it drank itself drunk and slowed down to have any chance of capturing it.

Incredibly wary, the Liquor worm waited until Fang Yuan retreated two hundred paces before leaping into the air, curling into a ball like a tiny white rice dumpling. The little dumpling arced through the air and landed on the grass sprinkled with green bamboo wine.

Enticed by the wine, the Liquor worm lowered its guard and eagerly plunged its head into a wine-filled flower bud, leaving only its chubby tail outside.

Fang Yuan stifled a laugh and waited patiently. While rushing forward offered a strong chance of catching it, he wanted the Liquor worm to lead him to the Flower Wine Monk's remains.

Once satiated, it curled into a ball and rose about a meter and a half off the ground, drifting leisurely toward the deeper bamboo forest.

Fang Yuan quickly followed after its trail.

Though heavily drunk and flying at half speed, the Liquor worm still forced Fang Yuan to run with all his might to keep up.

Pursuing it along the mountain stream, Fang Yuan heard the roar of a waterfall. Rounding a sparse thicket, he saw the Liquor worm fly into a crevice in a large boulder.

Fang Yuan's eyes lit up as he stopped.

"So it's here." He panted heavily, his heart pounding wildly and sweat drenching his body as blood surged through his veins.

After scanning his surroundings, Fang Yuan steadied his breath. With a resolute flash in his eyes, he took a deep breath and plunged headfirst into the crevice.

The boulder gap was wide enough for two adults to walk side by side, let alone a fifteen-year-old like Fang Yuan.

Freezing water drenched Fang Yuan as he pushed through the heavy current. After a few dozen steps, the pressure lessened.

Yet the fissure narrowed, forcing Fang Yuan to walk sideways. Roaring water filled his ears, white spray churned overhead, and pitch darkness lay deeper in the boulder.

Twenty steps later, he spotted a faint reddish glow ahead in the darkness.

Feeling the wall curve away into an opening, he took a few more steps and stepped into the light.

Before his eyes lay an enclosure of roughly eighty square meters.

"I must be inside the mountain cliff now," Fang Yuan thought, sizing up the hidden space.

A dim red glow filled the room, where withering vines clung to damp, mossy stone.

"These are Wine Sack Flower Gu and Rice Pouch Grass Gu." A thought struck him as he recognized the withering stems and vines.

Both were Rank one Gu; once mature, the flower secreted nectar wine while the grass pouch produced fragrant rice.

In a corner, atop a clump of withered roots, the Liquor worm slept soundly within easy reach.

Fang Yuan walked over, scooped up the Liquor worm, then knelt to pull apart the dead vines, revealing a bundled skeleton inside.

"I've finally found you, Flower Wine Monk." A smile touched his lips as he saw this.

Just as he reached out to clear the remaining vines, suddenly—

"Try touching it?" A voice full of murderous intent suddenly sounded behind Fang Yuan.

# Chapter 15: History is written by the victorious

In this secret cave, a voice suddenly loomed behind him.

The deep murderous intent made Fang Yuan groan inwardly; at Rank one initial stage with no vital Gu, he had zero combat ability.

"You have already been poisoned by my Single Gate Poison Gu. Without its counterpart, after seven days you will turn into pus and blood and die," the voice said behind him.

Fang Yuan gritted his teeth, his expression cold. He said in a low tone, "You want the Liquor worm? I can give it to you."

As he rose cautiously, another trembling voice cried out, "I'll give you anything, please just spare my life, O Flower Wine Monk!"

Turning in realization, Fang Yuan saw fluctuating light and shadow coalescing into an image on the wall.

On the wall, a menacing Gu Master stood over another prostrated before him amidst battle rubble.

The standing Gu Master threw his head back and laughed. "Gu Yue's hero, Rank five at such a young age—utterly unbearable!"

"The Flower Wine Monk!" Fang Yuan immediately recognized him.

"Compared to Sir Flower Wine, I am nothing! I must have been out of my mind to offend such greatness. Sir Flower Wine, please remember my clan's earlier hospitality and spare my life!"

Narrowing his eyes, Fang Yuan recognized the Gu Yue clan head's uniform—this groveling man was unmistakably the fourth generation clan leader!

"Hospitality? I came to trade fairly for your clan's moon orchids, yet you harbored evil intentions—feigning hospitality and inviting me to a banquet, only to poison my liquor with a poisonous Gu. You looked down on me too much. I have made my name under heaven as Flower Wine; how could I possibly be poisoned this way?"

Pointing at the kneeling clan leader, the Flower Wine Monk sneered, "Had you traded fairly, none of this would have happened. You wanted my head to boost your fame, so you have only yourself to blame for your death!"

"My clan has a spirit spring and vast moon orchids. I am willing to accept an Enslavement Gu and serve you for life!"

The Flower Wine Monk's anger calmed as his eyes narrowed. "Hmph, an Enslavement Gu is a precious Rank five Gu—do you really think I would have one? However, you have been infected by my Single Gate Poison Gu, which only I can cure. Your clan must deliver 3,000 stalks of moon orchids and 3,000 primeval stones every week. I will return periodically to collect the goods and temporarily suppress your poison, sparing your useless life."

"Stop kowtowing! A supposed Rank five genius, utterly unworthy of the name. Serve me well if you value your—Urgh!" The Monk cried out.

Kicking the fourth generation clan leader away, his body swayed as he stumbled back several steps, shouting, "How do you still have Gu?"

The fourth generation clan leader spat blood and struggled up with a cunning smile. "Anyone has the right to punish demonic scoundrels! This is the Moonshadow Gu, a master of concealment. Though only Rank four, it restricts the Primeval Sea and primeval essence. Demon, after our fierce battle you have few Gu left—how could you restrain the Moonshadow Gu? Surrender obediently and serve me well, and you might still live!"

The Flower Wine Monk flew into a rage and roared, "To hell with you!!"

Before his voice faded, he surged forward like lightning and punched straight into the fourth generation clan head's heart.

The tremendous punch hurled the fourth clan leader through the air, crashing down like a broken sack.

"Have you gone mad? We could have settled this..." Glaring at the Flower Wine Monk, his words faltered as his legs gave way, his head crooked, and he died.

The watching clan elders roared in fury and swarmed the area.

"Hahaha, all who seek death, come!" the Flower Wine Monk cried, charging headfirst into the oncoming elders.

The Flower Wine Monk overwhelmed the swarming elders, but just as he moved to finish them, he clutched his abdomen in pain. "Damn!"

"I'll return to deal with you lot," the Flower Wine Monk spat. Glaring at the surviving elders, he bolted like lightning into the mountain woods, disappearing in the blink of an eye.

# Chapter 16: Taking as much as possible that one can take

As the recording looped on the wall, Fang Yuan remained silent. When it repeated for the third time, he sighed faintly. "I see."

This audiovisual recording was likely left by the Flower Wine Monk using a Photo-audio Gu, which could record scenes and project them later.

Tearing away the withered vines had probably triggered the Photo-audio Gu hidden in the wall. It took little deduction to realize the footage was authentic.

The fourth generation clan leader had plotted against the Flower Wine Monk and failed; though his sneak attack repelled the monk, he died as a result. Because this history was disgraceful, the surviving clan elders had covered up the truth.

Yet the official story had a major loophole: if the clan claimed the Flower Wine Monk died on the spot, why were his remains here?

"I went through great trouble finding this treasure, so I will claim everything myself. Why share? So what if I'm discovered? Without braving risks, how can one gain profit? That Gu Master was truly cowardly," Fang Yuan sneered. Ignoring the repeating projection, he turned and used his strength to tear apart the dead roots and vines.

The intact skeleton broke into pieces under his pulling. Hardly caring, Fang Yuan kicked an obstructive leg bone aside and squatted to search the remains.

First he found a pouch containing only fifteen primeval stones.

After the fierce battle and poisoning, the Monk had likely consumed his primeval stones to heal; fifteen was already fortunate.

Next, he found several dead Gu carcasses. Mostly botanical varieties, they had withered away; as living creatures, Gu required food to survive, and most were picky eaters.

Over three hundred years, his Gu had perished, leaving only the Photo-audio Gu on the wall and the Liquor worm.

"The Liquor worm was the only real gain here—no wonder that Gu Master reported it. The profit was too meager to justify the risk."

In his memories, that Gu Master was already Rank three, making a Rank one Liquor worm virtually worthless to him, yet invaluable to Fang Yuan.

"Should I report this to the clan?" Fang Yuan briefly considered, then dismissed the idea.

After thoroughly scouring every corner of the cave, Fang Yuan retraced his steps home.

None of that mattered now. Though the Flower Wine Monk's modest treasure was somewhat disappointing, it made sense. Crucially, Fang Yuan had secured both his primary goal—the Liquor worm—and the primeval stones he desperately needed.

"Next, I must hole up in the inn to refine this Gu. With a vital Gu, I can return to the academy and qualify for a dorm room. I can also draw upon clan resources to cultivate. I can only afford the inn for a night or two before it becomes too costly." Pondering this, he hurried back to the village.

# Chapter 17: Starting to refine the Liquor worm

"With my C-grade talent, my aperture holds only 44% primeval sea. Since Gu consume essence far faster than my recovery rate, refining one requires external aid from primeval stones."

"Refining the Liquor worm will take eleven to sixteen stones." Though also Rank one, the Liquor worm was far rarer than the Moonlight Gu, making it much harder to refine.

Under the moonlight, Fang Yuan returned to the shuttered inn and banged on the door.

Dismissing the worker, Fang Yuan latched the door, washed up, and sat on the bed.

Though weary, excitement surged within him. "The Liquor worm effectively elevates a Gu Master's talent!"

Sitting cross-legged, Fang Yuan took out the sleeping Liquor worm. Slightly larger than a Moonlight Gu, it was soft and white like a silkworm.

Light in his palm, it gave off a delicate, natural wine aroma.

The fragrance entered his primeval sea, refining a gleam of higher-stage primeval essence.

Where his normal essence was emerald green, this refined essence was condensed pale green—the essence of Rank one middle stage.

Sensing the pale green essence in his primeval sea, Fang Yuan smiled. "My cultivation is only at Rank one initial stage right now. Yet with the Liquor worm's refinement, I will possess Rank one middle stage primeval essence. The value of this advantage is beyond words."

"However, only by refining it into my vital Gu can I freely purify my essence."

He drew a stream of primeval essence, wrapping it around the Liquor worm to invade its body.

Sensing danger, the Liquor worm woke instantly, thrashing violently as it tried to expel Fang Yuan's primeval essence.

"Its resistance is fierce." Fang Yuan's expression turned grave as his essence drained at more than double the rate of the Moonlight Gu.

"I must refine it, no matter what." Determination flashed in his eyes as he steadily poured primeval essence into the Liquor worm.

After half a night's effort, the Liquor worm's body bore only a faint tint of green covering less than one percent.

Fang Yuan's expression darkened. The Liquor worm's will was extraordinarily tenacious, showing resistance far beyond the norm for a Rank one Gu.

"This Gu was most probably the Flower Wine Monk's vital Gu. As a Rank five master's Gu, this Liquor worm was originally Rank five, but years of starvation caused its grade to fall. Now it is down to Rank one, yet its will remains tough as rock!"

"Refining it in a month is impossible without a master using a Rank two or three Gu's aura to suppress its inner will. Only with such help could I succeed with half the effort." Fang Yuan sighed.

More critically, he could not expose the Liquor worm.

Gu Yue Village had no Liquor worms, and Fang Yuan could not explain its origin.

"Seventeen primeval stones won't be enough; I need at least thirty. Troublesome, but I am determined to refine it." Fang Yuan's resolve was like steel.

Fang Yuan headed down to the dining hall to eat breakfast and plan how to gather more stones.

Just as he began eating, Gu Yue Fang Zheng appeared.

"Brother, why are you staying at an inn instead of sleeping at home?" his brother demanded bluntly.

# Chapter 18: Let the past disperse away like smoke

Fang Yuan said nothing and continued eating. He knew Fang Zheng lacked composure.

Seeing his brother ignore him like air, Fang Zheng snapped resentfully, "Brother, what did you do to Shen Cui? Ever since leaving your room yesterday, she's been crying nonstop. When I comforted her, she cried even harder!"

Stung by Fang Yuan's blatant contempt, Fang Zheng slammed the table and roared in frustration, "Gu Yue Fang Yuan, how can you act like this! Shen Cui has served you devotedly for years. I know you feel dejected, and that you are only a C grade talent, but you cannot vent your bitterness on her! It isn't fair!"

Before he finished, Fang Yuan rose, his hand flashing out.

With a loud snap he gave Fang Zheng a solid smack.

Clutching his right cheek, Fang Zheng stumbled back in shock.

"Useless bastard, what tone is that to use with your brother?!" Fang Yuan reprimanded in a low voice. "For a lowly servant girl, you forget I am your older brother?"

Staring wide-eyed through waves of stinging pain, Fang Zheng gasped in disbelief, "Brother, you hit me? You never hit me once growing up! Yes, I have A grade talent and you only C grade, but you cannot blame me for heaven's arrangement…"

Before Fang Zheng could finish, Fang Yuan backhanded him across the face.

Fang Zheng held both cheeks, utterly stunned.

"Have you forgotten how I cared for you? When aunt and uncle gave us one new robe for New Year, who wore it? When you wanted sweet porridge, I had the kitchen make extra. When others bullied you, who stood up for you? Yet now, because of a maid, you come questioning me?"

"Whatever," Fang Yuan sneered. "Since you even abandoned your biological parents to acknowledge others, what am I worth to you as a brother?"

"Brother, how can you say that? You know I've always longed for family warmth, I—" Fang Zheng stammered.

Fang Yuan waved a dismissive hand. "From today on, you are no brother of mine, and I am no longer your older brother."

"Brother!" Fang Zheng was surprised, opening his mouth to say more.

"Don't you like Shen Cui?" Fang Yuan said. "Don't worry, she is untouched. Give me six primeval stones and she is yours as your personal maid."

"Brother, why are you..." Having his inner thoughts suddenly exposed, Fang Zheng panicked.

Seeing his silence, Fang Yuan frowned. "Affection is normal; be honest. If you don't want to trade, that's fine too."

Fang Zheng grew anxious. "I'll trade! But I don't have six whole primeval stones left."

His face bright red, he took out his money pouch.

Fang Yuan took the pouch and found six stones, though one was reduced by half. Fang Zheng had clearly absorbed essence from it to speed up refining his Moonlight Gu; the more essence drawn from a stone, the smaller and lighter it became.

Though only five and a half stones, they were all Fang Zheng possessed, given to him by Aunt and Uncle.

"I'll keep these. You can leave," Fang Yuan said coldly, pocketing the pouch.

"What! He really said that?" In the hall, Uncle frowned coldly, while Aunt stared speechlessly at the red handprint on Fang Zheng's cheek.

"Yes, I met him having breakfast at the inn. That is what happened," Fang Zheng replied politely.

After a pause, Uncle sighed solemnly. "Fang Zheng, remember this: Shen Cui was assigned to him, not his personal property. How could he trade her? If you wanted her, you should have told us earlier, and we would have assigned her to you."

Uncle waved his hand. "Take your leave. I'll give you another six primeval stones. Use them well to refine your Gu and seize first place. Make us proud."

Once Fang Zheng left, Uncle's expression twisted in fierce anger.

He slammed the table with force, hissing, "Hmph, damn bastard. Trading our servant for profit—how cunning!"

Aunt advised, "Calm yourself, husband. It's only six primeval stones."

"What do you know! With C grade talent, six primeval stones would never have been enough to refine the Moonlight Gu. But with twelve, it is more than sufficient!" Uncle gritted his teeth in fury.

"With sufficient resources, a Gu Master can reach Rank two in two to three years. The lower Fang Yuan's cultivation, the less hope he has of claiming the inheritance in a year. We must hinder him early. Academy resources go to top students. Once he falls behind, he will receive nothing and lag even further. Trapped in that vicious cycle, let's see him try to claim the inheritance in a year!"

Aunt asked, "Even unhindered, he would reach Rank one middle stage at most. Husband, you are Rank two—why fear him?"

Uncle stomped in exasperation. "Short-sighted woman! As an elder, can I openly suppress a junior? His claim to the inheritance is legitimate; I can only thwart him through clan rules. To head the household at sixteen, clan rules require reaching Rank one middle stage, or he loses all right to the assets. After I have said this, do you understand now?"

Uncle narrowed his eyes with a sigh. "Fang Yuan is too cunning, seeing right through my maneuvers. What terrifying intellect! I intended to use Shen Cui to monitor and harass him, but he left and extorted six primeval stones instead."

"Treat Fang Zheng better from now on—he is an A grade talent and clearly resents Fang Yuan. Guided properly, that resentment will make him our finest tool to deal with Fang Yuan!"

In the blink of an eye, two days had passed.

Inside the unlit room, frosted by moonlight, Fang Yuan sat cross-legged on the bed, channeling his green copper primeval essence to refine the Liquor worm. A small patch was already dyed green, yet the Liquor worm's will remained tenacious, struggling ceaselessly amidst his primeval essence.

"Two days and nights, twelve primeval stones spent, yet I've only made one-fifteenth progress. Someone will succeed soon."

With his poor talent and the Liquor worm's stubborn will—far stronger than a Moonlight Gu's—falling behind was natural.

"Falling behind temporarily is nothing, so long as I obtain the Liquor worm..." Unperturbed and calm, Fang Yuan watched as the Liquor worm suddenly curled into a ball.

"A counterattack!" Fang Yuan opened his eyes in surprise. The Liquor worm had curled into a round dumpling, blazing with blinding white light.

It was risking everything in this one last stand!

A fierce will erupted from the Liquor worm, coursing along the primeval essence down into the Primeval Sea within his aperture.

Surprised but calm, Fang Yuan felt delighted. "A desperate last stand is good. Weathering this will break its will. However I need to put full focus into fighting back against this will, I cannot receive even the slightest outside interference. Or else that would be bad, sigh… But I hope no one will come and disturb me during this period."

Resolute, he gathered the primeval essence in his aperture, preparing to clash head-on with the Liquor worm's will.

But at this moment, a miraculous event happened!

High above the sea in the middle of his aperture, a Gu appeared.

The aura cascaded like a mountain deluge—a terrifying beast opening scarlet eyes upon intruders!

"The Spring Autumn Cicada?!" Seeing this Gu, Fang Yuan was utterly shocked!

# Chapter 19: Rank six vital Gu, The Spring Autumn Cicada!

Carrying the Flower Wine Monk's formidable will, the Liquor worm invaded his aperture in a brazen counterattack.

The willpower surged down toward the green copper Primeval Sea, whipping its surface into violent tides. At Fang Yuan's command, primeval essence gathered into a towering wave to meet the Liquor worm's incoming will.

Just as the two forces were about to collide, the faint silhouette of a Gu emerged between them.

It was a cicada of modest size, resembling a masterwork delicately carved from palm wood and tree leaves.

The Spring Autumn Cicada!

The aura expanded like an invisible tsunami, instantly swallowing the Liquor worm's helpless will whole.

With an echoing hum, the waves calmed as the cicada's aura bore down like an invisible mountain. The surface turned mirror-smooth, flattened as if an immense hand had smoothed out crumpled paper.

Though shocked, Fang Yuan felt no fear—only immense joy.

"The Spring Autumn Cicada actually followed me through rebirth! It isn't a single-use Gu, but reusable!"

The Spring Autumn Cicada was the sole Rank six Gu of his past life, taking thirty years of effort to refine.

Failing to find it after rebirth, he had assumed it perished; in reality, it had been slumbering deep within him.

Traveling five hundred years into the past had severely depleted its vitality, leaving it in a perilous condition.

Fang Yuan felt distressed that it teetered near death, yet also fortunate.

Had it been any stronger, he would be in grave danger.

A Gu Master and their Gu had to match in rank to complement each other.

At its peak, its majestic aura would have instantly shattered his fragile Rank one aperture.

Only in this weakened state could his aperture contain it.

"I searched tirelessly for the Liquor worm to make it my vital Gu, yet I had the Spring Autumn Cicada all along!" Fang Yuan felt a swell of emotion sensing the intimate bond between them.

"A Liquor worm is excellent, but compared to the Spring Autumn Cicada, it is mere dust!"

# Chapter 20: The academy elder is speechless

Unswayed by immense joy, he quickly calmed down and weighed the implications:

"Its ability is rebirth, but using it now would destroy it. Still, I can freely use its aura."

With a thought, Fang Yuan released a sliver of the cicada's aura onto the Liquor worm, freezing it motionless like a corpse.

Previously, the Liquor worm's fierce resistance had made refinement slow and arduous. Now, his green copper essence drove straight in without the slightest resistance.

With that, the Liquor worm was fully refined!

Withdrawing his essence, Fang Yuan watched the plump white Liquor worm leap through the air and plunge into his aperture. Inside, the Liquor worm circled warily around the hovering Spring Autumn Cicada before entering the green copper Primeval Sea.

Fang Yuan withdrew his focus and took out the Moonlight Gu, releasing a hint of the cicada's aura over it.

His essence poured in, instantly extinguishing the Moonlight Gu's will.

The Moonlight Gu settled onto his forehead, leaving a pale blue crescent mark between his brows.

"Ha ha, with the Spring Autumn Cicada at hand, it is as easy as having a god's help! After today all I just need to do is use its aura to pressure down, any Rank one Gu will be easily refined. Even with C grade talent, I no longer need primeval stones. The difference is like heaven and earth!"

"Time waits for no one—I must go claim the top prize at the academy!"

The Spring Autumn Cicada faded into slumber. He hid the Liquor worm under his bed to avoid scrutiny, then headed out.

Fifteen minutes later, in the clan academy.

The receptionist wore a stunned expression. "Wait, what did you just say? You are Gu Yue Fang Yuan, not Gu Yue Fang Zheng?"

Fang Yuan nodded. Just then, the elder entered, and both stood to greet him.

The academy elder strode over and patted his shoulder warmly. "You did well, Gu Yue Fang Zheng! You are truly an A grade genius! None of your B or C grade peers could ever compare, no matter how hard they try. Hahaha!"

Fang Yuan stepped back with hands folded behind his back, smiling faintly. "Sir elder, you are mistaken. I am Gu Yue Fang Yuan, Gu Yue Fang Zheng is my younger brother."

The startled elder gaped, frowning doubtfully. "You are Gu Yue Fang Yuan?"

"Correct sir," Fang Yuan replied.

"You've refined the Moonlight Gu?" The elder stared intently at the glowing crescent mark on his forehead.

"Indeed, that is the case," Fang Yuan said.

"Then, you are first in your batch?" The elder asked foolishly, but the situation was completely unexpected.

"If no one arrived before me..." Fang Yuan feigned deep thought, then rubbed his nose. "...then it seems so."

The academy elder: "....."

# Chapter 21: How Can It Be That Brother Got Number One?

"I cultivated hard and stayed up all night to refine the Moonlight Gu. As an A-grade talent, no one could be faster!"

He came to the academy entrance.

The two guards looked at him strangely. "Gu Yue Fang Yuan, why are you back?"

"What, brother was here just now?" Fang Zheng looked surprised and puzzled.

He cupped his hands arrogantly. "Brothers, I am Gu Yue Fang Zheng, not Gu Yue Fang Yuan. I have refined my vital Gu and come to claim the top prize."

The left guard's eyes widened. "You look so alike! But you came too late. Late last night, your brother Gu Yue Fang Yuan met with the elder and took the top prize."

"My brother!" Fang Zheng's eyes widened in shock. "You're saying he got first place?"

"The academy elder confirmed it, and the list will be posted soon. Didn't your brother tell you?" the other guard added.

"Impossible!" Fang Zheng raged inwardly. "I worked so hard while he drank every day, yet he got first place?!"

A new list appeared on the academy bulletin wall: Fang Yuan, then Fang Zheng.

Around the same time, at the Chi family branch residence:

Clan elder Gu Yue Chi Lian frowned thoughtfully. "Someone, call Young Master Chi Cheng over."

Soon, Gu Yue Chi Cheng entered looking lost and knelt respectfully. "Grandson greets Grandpa."

"It seems you've heard the news," Gu Yue Chi Lian said gently to his only direct grandson. "I called you so this wouldn't discourage you. In refining a vital Gu, talent comes first, but the Gu worm itself comes second."

"Fang Yuan taking first place with C grade talent was pure luck—his Moonlight Gu had a weaker will. His cultivation path will be far harder. Trust Grandpa, you will soon surpass him."

"Put this trivial matter aside. Fang Yuan is not worthy to be your rival; your real opponents are the A grade Fang Zheng and the Mo family's Mo Bei. Understand?"

"Thank you for your guidance, Grandpa. I understand! I will resume refining my Gu at once!" Chi Cheng's gloom vanished, replaced by high fighting spirit.

Elder Chi Lian nodded with a satisfied smile. "Rest assured Grandpa will support you fully. Later, I will use a Rank three Gu worm's aura to suppress your Moonlight Gu's will and help you refine it!"

# Chapter 22: Dancing Moonblade

Ten days had passed since Fang Yuan took first place in refining his vital Gu, and spring swept across the lush mountain.

Fifty-seven fifteen-year-old youths stood in a semi-circle on the training ground, focusing on the academy elder in their center.

"The Moonlight Gu is our Gu Yue clan's symbolic Gu. Most of you chose it as your vital Gu, so watch properly. I will demonstrate how to attack with it. This ranged attack method applies broadly to other Gu as well."

The academy elder opened his right hand with fingers spread wide, lowering his palm so the youths could see its center.

"First, mobilize the Moonlight Gu to your palm." As he spoke, the crescent mark shifted into his palm.

"Next, pour primeval essence into the Gu." A thread of white silver essence flowed into the Moonlight Gu in his palm.

Only Rank three Gu Masters produced white silver essence; Rank one was green copper, and Rank two red iron.

Absorbing the white silver primeval essence, the crescent mark glowed brightly, emitting a brilliant pale-blue light even in daytime.

The pale blue light flickered like scooped moonlight. The elder smiled. "Now watch: the final step is launching it."

Closing his spread fingers, he extended his arm straight forward and lightly waved his palm in a cutting motion.

The light formed a crescent moonblade, slicing through the air to strike a grass puppet ten meters away.

The moonblade cleanly severed the puppet's thick neck, sending its head tumbling to the ground.

Only Fang Yuan stood calmly in the crowd, his expression cold.

"Those who have refined the Moonlight Gu, step forward! Face a grass puppet and practice launching moonblades just as I demonstrated."

Thirty-five students soon formed a line, each facing a grass puppet ten meters away.

Fang Yuan stood unnoticed in the middle of the line as practice began.

The students moved the Moonlight Gu into their palms, pouring in green copper primeval essence.

Yet when they made the cutting motion, only seven or eight crescents flew out. Some vanished almost instantly, while others flew two or three meters before bursting into blue light.

The youths frowned; launching an accurate moonblade was far harder than the elder made it look.

The academy elder began guiding each student personally.

He focused on talents like Fang Zheng, Mo Bei, and Chi Cheng, sparing only a few words for C-grade students like Fang Yuan.

Fang Yuan gathered blue light in his palm and made mock cuts without releasing it, simply putting on an act. With the field in chaos, Fang Yuan tilted his palm slightly and made a cutting motion.

To avoid drawing attention, he aimed at the puppet to his left rather than the one opposite him.

With a whoosh, a moonblade sliced straight through the chaos and struck cleanly into a grass puppet's neck.

The grass puppet swayed under the deep cut, but the severed grass quickly began regrowing, intertwining to heal the wound.

This was no normal scarecrow, but a Rank one Scarecrow Gu possessing natural self-recovery.

Hits were rare, and Fang Yuan's strike caused the deepest damage so far, prompting cries of surprise. Even the academy elder took notice and asked, "That moonblade just now was not bad. Was it yours?"

He looked inquiringly at a C-grade student standing directly opposite that puppet.

The male student blinked in bewilderment, unsure in the chaotic flurry if he had thrown it.

Seeing the target, he thought it was probably him and nodded subconsciously.

"Well done. Keep trying hard and hold onto that feeling," the elder encouraged, patting his shoulder with a smile.

"Practice well after class; in three days I will test you," the elder announced. "Whoever performs best will receive ten primeval stones."

"Yes!" the students shouted, growing even more excited at the mention of primeval stones.

Yet just three minutes later, the flying moonblades began to thin.

# Chapter 23: Raising a Gu is like raising a mistress

Fang Yuan went to the Gu room alone.

The academy's Gu room kept various Rank one Gu worms, including the Moonlight Gu.

Students periodically got a free chance to pick a Gu worm, but additional Gu required payment.

With no desire to refine another Gu for now, Fang Yuan walked to the small shop next door.

"Junior, it's you." The twenty-something Rank one Gu Master running the shop greeted Fang Yuan while bargaining with customers.

Fang Yuan recognized Jiang Ya, the young Gu Master from the inn.

"Senior," Fang Yuan acknowledged with a nod, his expression blank.

Jiang Ya handed a grass puppet to a student, then smiled warmly. "Did Junior Brother come to buy a grass puppet as well? If you want me to save one, it's only three primeval stones. They sell fast; only seven are left, so don't wait too long."

Fang Yuan shook his head, secretly amused. Grass puppets made with the Scarecrow Gu cost at most one and a half primeval stones to make.

"Are you sure you don't want one?" Jiang Ya chuckled. "It looks like they're sold out, but I have an eighth stashed under the chest. Buy now or miss your chance!"

Uninterested in the puppets, Fang Yuan shook his head and placed a primeval stone on the counter. "I want ten moon orchid petals."

Jiang Ya took the stone and retrieved a paper bag from a drawer. "Ten moon orchid petals. Please check."

Fang Yuan checked the goods on the spot, confirmed the count, and left the shop.

A Gu Master must refine Gu, use Gu, and raise Gu.

Refining Gu risks backlash, using Gu takes practice, and raising Gu is complex given their varied diets.

The Moonlight Gu ate two moon orchid petals twice daily, while the Liquor worm drank wine. A jar of wine lasted four days, while the Spring Autumn Cicada fed directly on the River of Time.

Fang Yuan headed to the inn for green bamboo wine. The Liquor worm could survive on cheaper wine, but needed far greater quantities, making green bamboo wine more cost-effective and would not arouse suspicion.

Handing three primeval stones to the inn worker, Fang Yuan said, "A jar of green bamboo wine and a few dishes. Keep the change for now. At the end of the month when it amounts to a stone, subtract it from my bill."

Eating, Fang Yuan calculated in his mind: "One primeval stone buys ten petals, and the Moonlight Gu consumes four daily. Wine costs two stones every four days, meaning both Gu cost nearly one stone daily."

A mortal family lived on one stone a month. In sixteen days, Fang Yuan had already spent fourteen and a half primeval stones just on raising them.

"Between the Flower Wine treasure, Fang Zheng's bag, and the first place reward, my assets once reached forty-four and a half primeval stones. Refinement wasted six and a half stones, feeding took fourteen and a half, and living costs left me with twenty."

Fang Yuan checked his pouch of greyish-white primeval stones.

With twenty pieces left, he could only last another half month. Unlike his peers backed by wealthy relatives—like Gu Yue Mo Bei and Chi Cheng—

Fang Yuan could only think of a way himself.

"Uncle and Aunt cut off my living expenses, though the academy provides a three-stone weekly subsidy to every student. I must excel in the moonblade assessment to claim that ten primeval stone prize."

"Young sir! The merchant caravan arrives next month and always buys up our green bamboo wine. Since you buy several jars a week, the innkeeper told me to inform you. Our stock is limited, so we will have very little left after selling to them."

Fang Yuan needed a steady supply of wine to feed the Liquor worm without arousing suspicion.

He handed over ten primeval stones. "Then I'll buy five jars. Deliver them to my dormitory."

Petals spoiled quickly, but green bamboo wine kept for a long time.

After the jars were stashed under his dormitory bed, Fang Yuan looked at his flattened money pouch with a sigh.

In theory, a Gu Master could refine unlimited Gu if capable.

Thus, a common saying went: raising a Gu is like raising a mistress.

As rank rises, feeding requirements increase, so a Gu Master generally keeps only four or five Gu.

# Chapter 24: Close Combat Gu Master

Three days later.

"Lowering your body to dodge is the standard counter to an incoming fist. When an enemy attacks, quickly squat and counter by striking their crotch and abdomen. Do not fear a swinging fist. Usually, those who rush in swinging their fists immediately are brainless, impulsive, and rash."

On the martial arts field, the instructor demonstrated on a wooden puppet, dodging its swinging fist and knocking it down with swift counterpunches.

"Martial arts fundamentals are of utmost importance, especially in the early stages of a Gu Master's cultivation. All of you, focus your attention on me!"

The instructor stood up, his breathing calm and steady. He explained, "When facing a tall enemy in close combat, do not fear. Breaking their center of gravity is a sensible way to pin them down. Just as I did, seize the waist, control their hips, and drive forward with your strength. Then take the chance to mount them and punch fiercely. Anyone lacking defense will instantly collapse."

"This instructor is pragmatic, but his teaching is flawed." Watching quietly, Fang Yuan noted the lack of structure in the lessons. Consequently, the disorganized instruction soon caused most students to lose interest and drift off.

Only Fang Yuan listened meticulously, using the class to review and verify his five centuries of combat insights.

A Gu Master's combat style is typically divided into melee and ranged. The moonblade was a ranged attack, though strictly speaking medium-range, with an effective reach of only ten meters.

Melee Gu Masters typically cultivated Gu that enhanced their physical attributes, granting superhuman strength, agility, and endurance.
